For
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
, by changing
{\bf B},{\bf B}^{I},{\bf B}^{I}_{\log}

to
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}},{\bf B}^{I}_{S^{\flat}},{\bf B}^{I}_{S^{\flat},\log}
, we obtain Hyodo-Kato modules and sheaves:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r),\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r).

These are functors on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
.
In the case
X
is partially proper, we have analogs

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r)
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r)
for Hyodo-Kato cohomology with compact support
11
11

11

See
[
1
, Sec. 3, Sec. 4, Sec. 5]
for the definition and basic properties of compactly supported Hyodo-Kato cohomology.
and the following analog of quasi-isomorphism (
3.5
):

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
r\in{\bf Z}
. We have a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r))\simeq[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{\bf B}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}.

3.2.2.
Hyodo-Kato map

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
.
Recall that we have the natural Hyodo-Kato maps (see
[
21
, Sec. 4]
) in
{\mathcal{D}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})
and
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
, respectively:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Combined with the canonical map

\iota:{\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{\log}\to{\bf B}^{[u,v]}/t^{i}
, it defines a map between complexes of solid

{\bf B}^{[u,v]}
-modules:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{C},r)=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{\log}]^{N=0}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C},r).

(3.7)

Since we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi\otimes\varphi}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v/p]}}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(3.8)

the map (
3.7
) clearly lifts to a map of
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\rm FF}
:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C},r)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C},r).

This Hyodo-Kato map descends to the level of nuclear quasi-coherent sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{FF}}
:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C},r)\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C},r).

Everything above has a version for compactly supported cohomologies (see
[
1
, Sec. 3.2.2]
for Hyodo-Kato morphisms), as well as for
S
-cohomologies, for
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
(varying functorially in
S
).

3.3.
Syntomic cohomology

We pass now to syntomic cohomology.

3.3.1.
Classical syntomic cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
. Let
r\in{\bf N}
. Consider the classical syntomic cohomology (ala Bloch-Kato)
(see
[
21
, Sec. 5.4]
)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\big[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{C}/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r}\big].

It satisfies the following comparison theorem:

Theorem 3.9
.

(Period isomorphism,
[
21
, Th. 6.9]
)

Let
r\in{\bf N}
. There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\alpha_{r}:\quad\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(3.10)

Moreover, it yields a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\displaystyle\alpha_{r}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\geq 2d.

Proof.

Only the second claim requires justification. For the usual cohomology, this follows from quasi-isomorphism (
3.10
) and the fact that the complexes
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
,
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
live in the
[0,2d]
-range. To see the latter fact in the case
X
is Stein, note that using (
3.3
) we get
H^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+},i}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
, for
i\geq d+1
. From this and (
3.10
) we get that

H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d+j))=0
, for
d+j\geq i\geq d+1,j\geq 1
, and then, by twisting, that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=0
, for
i\geq d+1
, as wanted. Now, for a general partially proper
X
, we need to add
d
for the analytic dimension of cohomology yielding the range
[0,2d]
, as wanted.

For the cohomology with compact support, we argue similarly but using (
3.4
) instead of (
3.3
) in the case
X
is Stein. The case of partially proper
X

follows from that by a (co)-Čech argument.
∎

The above has a version in families. Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
and let
r\in{\bf N}
. We have the classical (crystalline) syntomic cohomology in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\big[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}(S)]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{C}/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(S))/F^{r}\big]

(3.11)

It satisfies the following comparison theorem:

Theorem 3.12
.

(Period isomorphism in families,
[
21
, Cor. 7.37]
,
[
1
, Prop. 6.16]
)

Let
r\in{\bf N}
. There is a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})

\alpha_{r}:\quad\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(3.13)

Moreover, it yields a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})

\displaystyle\alpha_{r}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\geq 2d.

Proof.

The argument is analogous to the one used in the proof of Theorem
3.9
.
∎

3.3.2.
Variants of syntomic cohomology

We will need the following variant of syntomic cohomology in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\big[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)\big],\quad r\in{\bf N}.

(3.14)

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
r\in{\bf N}
. There is a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(3.16)

Moreover, it yields a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\geq d.

Proof.

Let

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}:=W(R^{\flat,+})\langle[p^{\flat}]/p^{u}\rangle[1/p].
Define yet another variant of syntomic cohomology in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,\infty]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\big[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{[u,\infty]}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)\big].

The three different variants of syntomic cohomology introduced above are linked via maps

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,\infty]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))}

induced by canonical maps
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}(S)\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}
,

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
, and
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}
(see
[
20
, Sec. 2.4.2]
).
We claim that the map
f_{1}
is a quasi-isomorphism and the map
f_{2}
is a quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
. To show that, it suffices to prove that the related maps

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{1}:\quad[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}(S)]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{[u,\infty]}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1},

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{2}:\quad[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{[u,\infty]}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}

are quasi-isomorphisms in the wanted ranges. Or, first dropping (naively)
N=0
and then
\log
on both sides, that so are the maps

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{1}:\quad[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}(S)]^{\varphi=p^{j}}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}]^{\varphi=p^{j}},\quad j\in{\bf Z};

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{2}:\quad\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}]^{\varphi=p^{s}}\to\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}]^{\varphi=p^{s}},\quad s=r-1,r.

Let us first look at the map
f^{\prime}_{1}
. Taking cohomologies in degree
i\geq 0
, we get maps

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{1}:\quad(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}(S))^{\varphi=p^{j}}\to(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]})^{\varphi=p^{j}}.

We used here
[
21
, Prop. 5.8]
. Since
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
and
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are a countable limit, resp. colimit, of finite rank
\varphi
-isocrystals over
\breve{C}
, we may assume that the Hyodo-Kato cohomology groups are finite rank. But then, since
\varphi({\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]})\subset{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}(S)\subset{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]}
, it is clear that
f_{1}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism, as wanted.

Concerning the map
f_{2}^{\prime}
, we first pass to cohomology in degree
i
and then assume that the Hyodo-Kato cohomology has finite rank as above. Let
j\in{\bf N}
. We then claim that the map

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{j\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{1-\varphi}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{j\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}

(3.17)

is surjective for
i\leq j
. Indeed, by Proposition
2.13
, the complex (
3.17
) computes the cohomology of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
on
X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
associated to
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{j\}
. Our claim now follows from the fact that the slopes of Frobenius on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
are
\leq i
(see
[
21
, proof of Prop. 5.20]
) hence the slopes of
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
are
\geq 0
and
H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{i})=0
, as wanted.

Similarly, we see that the map

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{j\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{1-\varphi}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{j\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}

(3.18)

is surjective for
j\geq d
using the fact that the slopes of Frobenius on
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are in the
[i-d,d]
range (use Poincaré duality for Hyodo-Kato cohomology to flip to the usual cohomology).

Now, it suffices to show that, for
i\in{\bf N},j\geq-1
, the map

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{2}:\quad(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,\infty]})^{\varphi=p^{j}}\to(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]})^{\varphi=p^{j}}

is an isomorphism. But in the case
S=C
this follows from
[
8
, Prop. 3.2]
and the general case reduces to that one using the fact that all our algebras are spectral.

The above arguments prove the quasi-isomorphism in (
3.16
) for the usual cohomology and we get the statement for the compactly supported cohomology from the case of usual cohomology by a colim argument.
Concerning the last sentence of our lemma, the above argument shows the case of compactly supported cohomology. For the usual cohomology, since the complex

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{+}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
lives in the
[0,2d]
range (see the proof of Theorem
3.9
) it suffices to show that
so does the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
. But here we can use the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.9
.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

Bosco in
[
10
, Th. 6.3]
considered the following variant of syntomic cohomology in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\operatorname{FF}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\big[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{\bf B}}(X_{S},r)]^{\varphi=1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)\big],\quad r\in{\bf N}.

Lemma 3.20
.

The canonical map
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
induces a morphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\operatorname{FF}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

This is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.15
, it suffices to show that the induced morphism

[H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{1-\varphi}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}]\to[H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{1-\varphi}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}]

is a quasi-isomorphism in the case
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
is of finite rank. But this follows from Proposition
3.6
.
∎

3.3.3.
Syntomic
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\rm FF}

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
.

Definition 3.21
.

Let
r\in{\bf N}
. Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
.

(1)

Set

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)].

This is a nuclear
\varphi
-module over
{\bf B}^{\rm FF}_{S^{\flat}}
. We call it a
syntomic module
. We have

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\varphi),

where

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)].

(2)

The (nuclear)
syntomic sheaves
on
X_{\operatorname{FF},S}
are defined by

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{FF}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))).

We have a distinguished triangle in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{{\rm FF},S^{\flat}})

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r).

(3.22)

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
r\geq 2d
. We have natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Proof.

The first quasi-isomorphism follows from Proposition
2.13
. The second quasi-isomorphism follows from the first one, the quasi-isomorphism (
3.16
), and Theorem
3.12
.
∎

4.
Pro-étale complexes on the Fargues-Fontaine curve

In this section we define quasi-coherent sheaves on the Fargues-Fontaine curve representing

p
-adic (geometric) pro-étale cohomology (with
{\bf Q}_{p}(r)
-coefficients)
of smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties
(Proposition
4.3
)
and prove a comparison theorem with the quasi-coherent sheaves representing syntomic cohomology
(Proposition
4.4
). This follows from a comparison theorem (Theorem
4.7
and Corollary
4.9
)
for the
{\mathbb{B}}
-period sheaf, which amounts
to a comparison theorem on
Y_{\rm FF}
instead of
X_{\rm FF}
, i.e., to untangling
comparison theorems from the action of
\varphi
(that this could be done with no much pain came to us as a surprise).

4.1.
Definitions

We start with definitions.

4.1.1.
Twisted coefficients

Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
.
Let
n,k\geq 0
. Define the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}(n,k)
on
X_{\rm FF,S^{\flat}}
by the exact sequence of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
-modules

0\to{\mathcal{O}}(n,k)\to{\mathcal{O}}(n)\to i_{\infty,*}({\mathcal{O}}/t^{k})\to 0,

where the first map is an inclusion. The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}(n,n)
will be the target of our trace maps. Note that
{\mathcal{O}}(n,k)
is just
{\mathcal{O}}(n-k)
with (Galois-)Tate twist
k
; in particular, we have
H^{0}(X_{\rm FF,S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{O}}(n,n))=\underline{{\bf Q}_{p}}(S)(n)
.

On the level of
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}
, the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}(n,k)
is the module
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{n,k\}
represented by the module

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n,k\}
defined by the exact sequence

0\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n,k\}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n\}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n\}/t^{k}\to 0,

(4.1)

where the first map is an inclusion. We have
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n,k\}\simeq{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n-k\}(k)
as a Frobenius, Galois module.
Note that the Frobenius map:

\varphi:{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n,k\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]},\varphi}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{n,k\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}

is an isomorphism because it is isomorphic to the Frobenius on
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\{n-k\}
.

4.1.2.
Pro-étale modules and sheaves

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper dagger variety over
K
.
For
r\in{\bf N}
,
v^{\prime}=v,v/p
, and
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
, we set

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v^{\prime}]}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]})(r),

where
{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}
denotes the relative period sheaf corresponding to
{{\bf B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}
(see
[
10
, Sec. 2.3.1]
for a description of condensed structure on these modules). We will need the following fact.

Lemma 4.2
.

The canonical map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}{\to}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

By pro-étale descent, it suffices to show that, for a set of perfectoid affinoids
\{S_{i}\},

i\in I
, the canonical map

(\prod_{I}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v]}})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\to\prod_{I}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v/p]}}

is a quasi-isomorphism. (We used here the fact that
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}({S_{i}},{\mathbb{B}}^{J})\simeq{\bf B}^{J}_{S^{\flat}}
.) But this follows from the fact that this tensor product commutes with derived limits (see the proof of Lemma
2.4
) and the canonical map

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v]}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v/p]}}

is an isomorphism. To see the last claim, we compute

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v]}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\simeq{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v]}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{({\bf Z}[T],{\bf Z})_{\Box}}({\bf Z}[T],{\bf Z}[T])_{\Box}\simeq{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}_{i}}^{{[u,v/p]}},

where we wrote

{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\simeq{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\langle f\rangle

for
f=(p/[p^{\flat}]^{p/v})\in{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
.
∎

We define the
pro-étale modules
as the pairs

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\varphi),

\displaystyle\varphi:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v/p]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

where the Frobenius
\varphi
is induced by the Frobenius
\varphi:{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]}
. It
yields a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v/p]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})

\varphi:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]},\varphi}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v/p]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Indeed, it suffices to show that the Frobenius map

\varphi:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]},\varphi}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v/p]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]})

is a quasi-isomorphism. But this follows directly from
[
10
, Lemma 4.8]
.

The pairs
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

defines nuclear
\varphi
-complexes (actually
(\varphi,{\mathcal{G}}_{K})
-complexes) over
{\bf B}^{\rm FF}_{S^{\flat}}
, which we will call
pro-étale modules
. For the nuclear property use
[
10
, Lemma 6.15]
plus preservation of nuclearity by countable products and finite limits for the usual cohomology.
The case of cohomology with compact support follows since colimits preserve nuclearity. We will denote by

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{FF}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)))

the corresponding nuclear quasi-coherent sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
. We will call them
pro-étale sheaves
.
Pro-étale modules and sheaves are functors on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

We have a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Proof.

By Proposition
2.13
we have natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)))

\displaystyle\simeq[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi-1}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}^{{[u,v/p]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))]

\displaystyle\simeq[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})(r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi-1}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]})(r)]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Here, in the last quasi-isomorphism, we have used the exact sequence (see
[
20
, Lemma 2.23]
)

\phantom{XXXXXXXXXXXXXXX}0\to{\bf Q}_{p}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi-1}}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]}\to 0\phantom{XXXXXXXXXXXXX}\qed

4.2.
Comparison theorems on the Fargues-Fontaine curve

We move now to the comparison theorems on the two curves of Fargues-Fontaine.

4.2.1.
Comparison theorem on the
X_{\rm FF}
-curve

W start with the "bottom" curve. Let
X
be a smooth partially proper variety over
K
, of dimension
d
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
r\geq 2d
.
There is a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
:

\alpha_{r}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(4.5)

Proof.

It suffices to construct a natural quasi-isomorphism of
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

That is, a natural quasi-isomorphism of pairs

({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\varphi)\simeq({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\varphi).

But this follows
from a "Frobenius untwisted" version of Theorem
3.12
presented in Theorem
4.7
below. We just have to argue that we can drop truncations in
4.8
: but this follows from the fact that both sides live in degrees
[0,2d]
, which can be seen as in the proof of Theorem (
3.9
)). ∎

Remark 4.6
.

We did not list the truncated version of Theorem
3.12
in Proposition
4.4
because the issue of truncation vis a vis localization is a subtle one.

4.2.2.
Comparison theorem on the
Y_{\rm FF}
-curve

We pass now to the "top" curve.

Theorem 4.7
.

(Comparison theorem on the
Y_{\operatorname{FF}}
-curve)

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper variety over
K
. Let
r\geq 0
. We have natural, functorial in
S
, and compatible with Frobenius quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})

and
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v/p]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
, respectively:

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})(r)

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)],

(4.8)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]})(r)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v/p]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r).

Proof.

For
v^{\prime}=v,v/p
, we define
F^{r}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}:=t^{r}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}
. We clearly have the isomorphism

t^{r}:{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F^{r}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v^{\prime}]}\{r\}.

We want to construct natural, functorial in
S
and compatible with Frobenius,
quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
and
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v/p]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
, respectively:

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},F^{r}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})\{r\}

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)],

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},F^{r}{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v/p]})\{r\}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v/p]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r).

For the usual cohomology, these quasi-isomorphisms were constructed in
[
21
, Sec. 7]
. They are not explicitly stated there because we almost always carry through the constructions the eigenspaces of Frobenius but, in fact, the latter can be dropped
as they are only used to pass between various period rings and here we work with one fixed period ring. For the gist of the construction the interested reader should consult the diagram (7.16) (with the top row moved a step lower and with added
[u,v]
-decoration), its refinement (7.31), Section 7.4 in general, and diagram (7.36) (with decoration changed again to
[u,v]
) in particular.

The case of compactly supported cohomology follows now easily from the case of usual cohomology by taking colimits and finite limits.
∎

The following result follows easily from the above theorem though it will not be used in this paper.

Corollary 4.9
.

(
{\mathbb{B}}
-comparison theorem)

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper variety over
K
. Let
r\geq 0
.

(1)

Let
I=[u,v]\subset(0,\infty)
be a compact interval with rational endpoints containing the fixed intervals from Section
2.2.2
. We have a natural, functorial in
S
, quasi-isomorphism
in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{I}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
:

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{I})(r)

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{I}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{I}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{I}(X_{S},r)].

This quasi-isomorpism is also compatible with Frobenius, i.e., the following diagram commutes (we set
u^{\prime}=u/p,v^{\prime}=v/p
)

\textstyle{\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{[u,v]}(X_{S},r)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]})(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]}(X_{S},r)].}

(2)

We have a natural, functorial in
S
, and compatible with Frobenius quasi-isomorphism

in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
:

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}})(r)

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq r}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)]

For
r\geq 2d
, this yields a quasi-isomorphism

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}})(r)\simeq[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}\prod_{I}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\mathbb{B}}(X_{S},r)].

(4.10)

Remark 4.11
.

(1) In claim (1) above, we have set

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}^{I}(X_{S},r):=\bigoplus_{{\bf Z}(I)}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}(S))/F^{r},

where
{\bf Z}(I):=\{n\in{\bf Z}|\varphi^{n}(y_{\infty})\in Y^{I}_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}\}
.
The Hyodo-Kato morphism
\iota^{I}_{\operatorname{HK}}
in degree
n\in{\bf Z}(I)
is defined by precomposing the usual Hyodo-Kato morphism
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}
with
\varphi^{-n}
. It is

{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}(S)
-linear via the composition

{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}(S)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi^{-n}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{[p^{n}u,p^{n}v]}(S)\to{\mathbb{B}}^{[p^{n}u,p^{n}v]}(S)/\xi_{\infty}^{r}\simeq{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}(S)/t^{r},

where
\xi_{\infty}
is a generator of the ideal defining
y_{\infty}
.

(2) In claim (2) above, we have set

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(X_{S},r)

\displaystyle:=\prod_{{\bf Z}}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}(S))/F^{r}),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\mathbb{B}}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)

\displaystyle:=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\mathbb{B}}_{\log}(S)]^{N=0}.

Proof.

The first quasi-isomorphism is proven in the same way as Theorem
4.7
. The second one follows from the first one by passing to limits (we use here heavily that we may assume the Hyodo-Kato cohomology to be of finite rank) once we know that
{\mathrm{R}}^{i}\lim_{I\subset(0,\infty)}{\mathbb{B}}^{I}(S)=0,i>0
, where the limit is taken over compact intervals
I
with rational endpoints (recall that we have
\lim_{I\subset(0,\infty)}{\mathbb{B}}^{I}(S)\simeq{\mathbb{B}}(S)
). But this was checked in
[
10
, proof of Lemma 2.41]
.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

(1) For
r\geq 2d
, as an immediate consequence of (
4.10
), we get the quasi-isomorphism :

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}})(r)[1/t]\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)[1/t].

(2) Bosco in
[
10
]
proved a version of the comparison quasi-isomorphism (
4.10
), where the torsion on the right-hand side is incorporated to the left-hand side via the
\operatorname{L}\eta_{t}
operator.

5.
Poincaré dualities on the Fargues-Fontaine curve

We are now ready to state and prove pro-étale duality on the Fargues-Fontaine curve. The same techniques allow us to prove also pro-étale Künneth formula.

5.1.
Hyodo-Kato and de Rham dualities

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
, of dimension
d
.

5.1.1.
De Rham dualities

Recall the following dualities (see
[
1
, Cor. 5.18, Th. 5.23, Cor. 5.26]
).

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
L=K,C
.

(1)

(Serre duality) There is a
trace map of solid
L
-modules

{\rm Tr}_{\rm coh}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{L},\Omega^{d})[d]\to L.

The pairing

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{L},\Omega^{j})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{L},\Omega^{d-j})[d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{L},\Omega^{d})[d]\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{\rm coh}}}L

is perfect, i.e., it yields the quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})
:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{L},\Omega^{j})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{L},\Omega^{d-j})[d],L).

(2)

(Filtered de Rham duality)
There are natural
trace maps in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})
and
L_{\Box}
, respectively:

{\rm Tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d]\to L,\quad{\rm Tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\,H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})\to L.

(a)

The pairing in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm Tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d],L).

(b)

More generally, let
r,r^{\prime}\in{\bf N},r+r^{\prime}=d
. The pairing in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})

({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L})/F^{r^{\prime}+1})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{L}F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{L})[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm Tr}_{\operatorname{dR}}}L

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have induced quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}(L_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L})/F^{r^{\prime}+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{L_{\Box}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)[2d],L),

\displaystyle F^{r^{\prime}+1}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{L_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)/F^{r}[2d],L).

5.1.2.
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-dualities

The duality for
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology has a slightly different form. For
r\geq d
, a natural trace map in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})
can be defined by the composition

{\rm Tr}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}:\quad F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\otimes_{K}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{\rm coh}\otimes{\rm Id}}}F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

Corollary 5.2
.

(Filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-duality
[
1
, Cor. 5.27]
)

Let
r,r^{\prime}\geq d,s=r+r^{\prime}-d
. The pairing in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d]\to F^{r^{\prime}+r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d]\xrightarrow{{\rm Tr}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}}F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

is a perfect duality, i.e., we have an induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

\displaystyle F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d],F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

We will need a variant of the above result. To state it, take
r,r^{\prime}\geq d,s=r+r^{\prime}-d
and consider the pairing in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r^{\prime}})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s})[2d-1]\to F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

(5.3)

defined as the composition

\displaystyle({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle/F^{r^{\prime}})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s})[2d-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cup}}{{\to}}F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s}[2d-1]

\displaystyle\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{d})\otimes_{K}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}(F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{s})[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X_{C},\Omega^{d})\otimes_{K}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{\rm coh}\otimes{\rm Id}}}F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

Here the third morphism is the boundary map induced by the exact sequence

0\to F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{can}}}F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to F^{r-d}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{s}\to 0

Corollary 5.4
.

The pairing (
5.3
) is a perfect duality, i.e., we have an induced quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box})

\displaystyle\gamma:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s}[2d-1],F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

(5.5)

Proof.

Consider the following map of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d],F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(t^{s}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})[2d],F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},\Box}}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s}[2d-1],F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

where the middle arrow is the de Rham duality map (
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-linearized) and the top arrow is the
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-duality map from Corollary
5.2
. Both are quasi-isomorphisms (see Proposition
5.1
). Hence so is the bottom duality map, as wanted.
∎

The duality map (
5.5
) can be lifted to the Fargues-Fontaine curve: the pairing (
5.3
) induces a pairing of
{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}
-modules

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s})\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{s,s\}[-2d+1],

which, in turn, induces a pairing of nuclear
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s})\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s,s\}[-2d+1],

where we set
F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s}:=(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s},0)
.
This descends to a pairing on
X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}i_{\infty,*}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s})\to{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)[-2d+1],

(5.6)

where we set
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{K})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}(S)
.
The pairing (
5.6
) induces a duality map in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
:

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathbb{D}}(i_{\infty,*}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s})[2d-1],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)),

(5.7)

where we set

{\mathbb{D}}(-,-):={\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})}(-,-).

Lemma 5.8
.

The duality map (
5.7
) is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We need to show that the duality map

\gamma^{\operatorname{FF}}_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s}[2d-1],{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s,s\})

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}})
. Or, passing to solid
{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
-modules, that the duality map

\gamma_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S})/t^{s}[2d-1],{\bf B}^{\prime})

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
. But this is Corollary
5.4
(strictly speaking, its
S
-version but it holds by the same arguments).
∎

5.1.3.
Hyodo-Kato duality

This is based on
[
1
, Sec. 5.4]
.
There exists a natural trace map in
{\mathcal{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})
:

{\rm Tr}_{X}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\to\breve{C}\{-d\}[-2d].

The pairing in
{\mathcal{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})
(
s=r+r^{\prime}-d
)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{r^{\prime}\}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{r+r^{\prime}\}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}\breve{C}\{s\}[-2d]

(5.9)

is perfect, i.e.,
it induces a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{r^{\prime}\},\breve{C}\{s\}[-2d]),

(5.10)

where the internal
{\rm{Hom}}
is just
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\breve{C}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C}),\breve{C}[-2d])
– the internal
{\rm{Hom}}
in
{\mathcal{D}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})
– equipped with

(\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K})
-actions via
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{r^{\prime}-s\}
.

The above duality can be lifted to the Fargues-Fontaine curve: the pairing (
5.9
) induces a pairing of
{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}
-modules

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})\to\breve{C}\{s\}\otimes_{\breve{C}}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}[-2d],

which, in turn, induces a pairing of nuclear
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})\to{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s\}[-2d].

This descends to a pairing on
X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{O}}(s)[-2d],

which induces a duality map in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
:

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s)).

(5.11)

Lemma 5.12
.

The map
\gamma_{X_{S}}
above is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
.

Proof.

Since
{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
is
{\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{S^{\flat},{\rm an}}
-complete (see
[
2
, Lemma 3.24]
), by Remark
2.6
,
we may pass from
{\bf B}^{[u,v],+}_{S^{\flat}}
to
{\bf Z}
, i.e., to
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}
-modules. Hence we need to show that the duality map

\gamma^{\operatorname{FF}}_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s\})

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}})
. Or, passing to solid

{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
-modules, that the duality map

\gamma_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime})

(5.13)

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
. We claim that, for that,
it suffices to check that, for
j\in{\bf N}
, the duality map on cohomology groups level

\gamma^{j}_{X_{S}}:H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r){\to}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}(H^{{[u,v]},2d-j}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime}),{\bf B}^{\prime})

(5.14)

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}
.
Indeed, passing to cohomology in (
5.13
), we need to check that the duality map

\gamma^{j}_{X_{S}}:H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r){\to}H^{j}({\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}
. But
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
is a direct sum of copies of
\breve{C}
hence we have

H^{j}({\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime}))\simeq\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}(H^{{[u,v]},2d-j}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime}),{\bf B}^{\prime}),

as wanted.

To prove (
5.14
), we observe that,
for
i\in{\bf Z}
, we have the natural isomorphisms
12
12

12

We can ignore the Galois action here.

H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},i)\simeq(H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}.

(5.15)

Here the second quasi-isomorphism is defined by the map
\exp(NU)
(this makes sense because the monodromy operator on the Hyodo-Kato cohomology

H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})
is nilpotent). For the first quasi-isomorphism

H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},i)=H^{j}([{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log}]^{N=0})\simeq(H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}

we used the fact that

H^{j}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})\simeq H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{i\}\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log},

that
N
is nilpotent on
H^{{[u,v]},j}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})
(so we can do devissage by the kernels of the action of
N
), and that
{\bf B}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log}]^{N=0}
.

It is easy to check that the maps in (
5.15
) are compatible with products. Hence we can write the duality map (
5.14
) as the Hyodo-Kato pairing

\gamma^{j}_{X_{S}}:H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}{\to}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}(H^{2d-j}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime},{\bf B}^{\prime}).

To show that it is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}
it suffices thus to evoke the Hyodo-Kato duality (
5.10
) and to show that the natural map

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\breve{C}_{\Box}}(H^{2d-j}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C}),\breve{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle{\to}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\breve{C}_{\Box}}(H^{2d-j}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C}),{\bf B}^{\prime})

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}_{\Box}^{\prime}
.
But this is an isomorphism by
[
30
, Th. 3.40]

since
{\bf B}^{\prime}
is a Banach space over
\breve{C}
.
∎

5.2.
Syntomic duality

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
of dimension
d
. Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
.
Recall that syntomic
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}
are defined as (see Sec.
3.3.3
)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)],

where the Hyodo-Kato map is described by diagram (
3.8
). The Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cup products are compatible with this diagram hence yield a cup product on the syntomic

\varphi
-modules:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime})).

This product can be described by an analogous product on the
{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
-chart:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime})).

(5.16)

It is compatible with the products on
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},i)
and
F^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})
.
Here we defined
F^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})
as
F^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
with
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
replaced by
{\bf B}^{\prime}
.

Let
s\geq d
. There is a trace map

{\rm Tr}_{X}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}\{s-d,s-d\}[-2d]

defined on the
{\bf B}^{\prime}
-chart via the trace map

{\rm Tr}^{[u,v]}_{X}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to{\bf B}^{\prime}\{s-d,s-d\}[-2d],

(5.17)

which is compatible with the Hyodo-Kato and de Rham trace maps.
The map
{\rm Tr}^{[u,v]}_{X}
is defined using the exact sequence

H^{{[u,v]},2d}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},s)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S},s),

which can be written more explicitly as the exact sequence

H^{{[u,v]},2d}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to(H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{s\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes_{K}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}({\bf B}^{\prime}\{s-d\}/F^{s-d}).

(5.18)

Using the (compatible) Hyodo-Kato and de Rham trace maps

{\rm Tr}_{X}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\{s\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\breve{C}\{s-d\},\quad{\rm Tr}_{X}:H^{2d}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}K,

(
5.18
) yields a map

H^{{[u,v]},2d}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s-d))\to{\rm Ker}({\bf B}^{\prime}\{s-d\}\to{\bf B}^{\prime}\{s-d\}/F^{s-d})={\bf B}^{\prime}\{s-d,s-d\},

hence the trace (
5.17
), as wanted.

For
s:=r+r^{\prime}-d
, the above can be lifted to the Fargues-Fontaine curve: the cup product (
5.16
) and trace map (
5.17
) induce a pairing of
{\bf B}^{\prime}
-modules

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cup}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}^{[u,v]}_{X}}}{\bf B}^{\prime}\{s,s\}[-2d],

which, in turn, induces a pairing of nuclear
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cup}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s,s\}[-2d].

This descends to a pairing in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cup}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)[-2d],

which induces a natural map
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)).

(5.19)

Theorem 5.20
.

(Syntomic Poincaré duality on the Fargues-Fontaine curve)

Let
r,r^{\prime}\geq 2d,s:=r+r^{\prime}-d
.
The map
\gamma_{X_{S}}
is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
.

Proof.

It is enough to show this in
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\rm FF}_{S^{\flat}}
for the corresponding map

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\rm FF}_{S^{\flat}}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s,s\}).

(5.21)

Or in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{S^{\flat},\Box})
for the induced map

\gamma^{[u,v]}_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime}(s)).

But for that, it is enough to check
that base changes of
\gamma^{[u,v]}_{X_{S}}
to both
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]
and
{\bf B}^{\prime}/t
are quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
. This last claim requires a bit of justification. We have the exact sequence of solid
{\bf B}^{\prime}
-modules

0\to{\bf B}^{\prime}\to{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]\to{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]/{\bf B}^{\prime}\to 0.

Hence it suffices to check that base changes of
\gamma^{[u,v]}_{X_{S}}
to both
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]
and
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]/{\bf B}^{\prime}
are quasi-isomorphisms. Writing
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]/{\bf B}^{\prime}=\operatorname{colim}_{n}({\bf B}^{\prime}/t^{n})
and using the fact that the tensor products commute with filtered colimits, we see that it suffices to check that base changes of
\gamma^{[u,v]}_{X_{S}}
to both
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]
and
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]/t^{i}
are quasi-isomorphisms. Finally, by devissage, we can drop
i
to
1
, as wanted.

For the first base change, we have quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))[1/t]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)[1/t],

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime})[1/t]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime}(s))[1/t].

And
\gamma^{[u,v]}_{X_{S}}
is just the canonical map

\gamma_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)[1/t]\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime}),{\bf B}^{\prime})[1/t]

induced by the Hyodo-Kato pairing (
5.9
). Since it is compatible with
t
-action, it suffices to show that
the canonical map

\gamma_{X_{S}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime}),{\bf B}^{\prime})

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
. But this was shown in (
5.13
), in the proof of Lemma
5.12
.

For the base change to
{\bf B}^{\prime}/t
, write
S={\rm Spa}(R,R^{+})
; then
{\bf B}^{\prime}/t=R
. We
claim that we have a compatible with product quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R.

(5.22)

To show
(
5.22
) we compute:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)]\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes{\rm Id}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R]

Then we use the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

It defines quasi-isomorphisms between the mapping fibers of the rows yielding (
5.22
). The quasi-isomorphism in the above diagram needs a justification: take the composition

({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C})\{s\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

It is equal to
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}
hence a quasi-isomorphism, as wanted.

From (
5.22
), we get the quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],R)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}(F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})[2d],R).

We have
quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
compatible with products
(see
[
1
, Prop. 3.6, Prop. 3.10]
)

\displaystyle F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\beta_{X}]{\sim}\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r-i)[-i],

(5.23)

\displaystyle F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\beta_{X}]{\sim}\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r^{\prime}-i)[-i].

Putting (

compatible with products

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r-i)[-i],

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{R_{\Box}}(\oplus_{i=0}^{d}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r^{\prime}-i)[2d-i],R).

And our result follows from Serre duality
13
13

13

Apply it in degree

.
(see Proposition
5.1
) which yields the quasi-isomorphisms in

,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{K_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-i})[d],K)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{R_{\Box}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{c}(X,\Omega^{d-i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R[d],R).

The second quasi-isomorphism holds by the same argument as the one used at the end of the proof of Lemma
