.
∎

5.3.
Syntomic duality: an alternative argument

We present here an alternative proof of Theorem
5.20
(conditional on the unchecked tedious compatibilities in Lemma
5.27
below).
It uses dual modifications to inverse the arrows in the defining syntomic distinguished triangles (
3.22
).

More precisely, let
j\geq i\geq 0
. We will construct a distinguished triangle in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},i)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(0,j)\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\to i_{\infty,*}F^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{j},

(5.24)

which is a twisted version of (
3.22
). To do that, consider the following map of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},i)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(0,j)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{i_{\infty,*}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S},i)}

(5.25)

Here, the bottom distinguished triangle is (
3.22
); the top one is induced from the distinguished triangle

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},i)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}_{S^{\flat},[u,v]}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}\{0,j\}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},i)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},i)/t^{j}

obtained by tensoring the exact sequence (
4.1
) for
0,j
with
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},i)
.
(Recall that
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{C},r)=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\{r\}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{S^{\flat},\log}]^{N=0}
). The dashed arrow in diagram (
5.25
) is defined to make the diagram a map of distinguished triangles.
The diagram yields quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},i)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(0,j)\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))][1]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}[i_{\infty,*}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{j}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S},i)]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{\infty,*}F^{i}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{j}.

That is, we get a distinguished triangle (
5.24
), as wanted.

Now, let
r,r^{\prime}\geq 2d,s=r+r^{\prime}-d
. Consider
the following diagram in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
(note that
s\geq r^{\prime}
)
whose columns are distinguished triangles

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\operatorname{syn}}_{X_{S}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\operatorname{HK}}_{X_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{S},r^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(0,s)[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{S},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\operatorname{dR}}_{X_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}}(i_{\infty,*}F^{r^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{S}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{s}[2d-1],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))}

(5.26)

where the horizontal maps are defined by the syntomic, Hyodo-Kato, and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-pairings,
respectively (see (
5.19
), (
5.11
), (
5.7
)).

Let us assume Lemma
5.27
below. To prove that the top horizontal arrow in diagram
5.26

is a quasi-isomorphism it suffices to show that so are the two lower arrows. But this follows
from Lemma
5.12
(we used the isomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}(0,s)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(s)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)
) and Lemma
5.8
.

Lemma 5.27
.

Diagram (
5.26
) above is a map of distinguished triangles.

5.4.
Pro-étale duality

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
of dimension
d
. Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
.
We define a cup product on the pro-étale

\varphi
-modules:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))

(5.28)

via the cup product on the
{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
-charts:

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]}(r+r^{\prime}))

induced by the cup product on pro-étale cohomology.
This product is compatible with the syntomic product (via the comparison quasi-isomorphism from Theorem
4.7
): to see this it suffices to argue for the usual cohomology and locally, where the comparison map is known to be compatible with products.

Let
s\geq 2d
. We define a trace map

{\rm Tr}_{X}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}\{s-d,s-d\}[-2d]

(5.29)

as the composition

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}\{s-d,s-d\}[-2d].

By
[
1
, Prop. 7.17]
, for
S=\operatorname{Spa}(C,{\mathcal{O}}_{C})
, this map is compatible with Huber’s trace map.

For
r,r^{\prime}\geq d,s:=r+r^{\prime}-d
, the above can be lifted to the Fargues-Fontaine curve: the cup product (
5.28
) and trace map (
5.29
) induce a pairing of nuclear
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cup}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{\bf B}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}\{s,s\}[-2d].

This descends to a pairing in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cup}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)[-2d],

which induces a natural map
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)).

(5.30)

By an abuse of notation, we will write

\gamma_{X_{S}}:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\mathcal{O}}).

(5.31)

for the Tate-untwisted version of the map (
5.30
).

Corollary 5.32
.

(Pro-étale Poincaré duality on the Fargues-Fontaine curve)

The map
\gamma_{X_{S}}
from (
5.31
) is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
.

Proof.

Choose
r,r^{\prime}\geq 2d
and set
s:=r+r^{\prime}-d
. It suffices to prove that the Tate twisted map (
5.30
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
This follows immediately from the syntomic duality from Theorem
5.20
and the comparison result from Proposition
4.4
.
∎

5.5.
Künneth formula

Let
X,Y
be smooth Stein rigid analytic varieties over
K
. The simple observation that we have a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}(K_{\Box})

\big(\Omega(X)\otimes_{K}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}{\mathcal{O}}(Y)\big)\oplus\big({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}\Omega(Y)\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Omega(X\times_{K}Y),

(5.33)

which implies the Künneth formula for de Rham cohomology

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\times_{K}Y)

leads to the syntomic Künneth formula in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF}})
and hence the pro-étale as well:

Theorem 5.34
.

(Künneth formula)
Let
X,Y
be smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties over
K
. Let
d
be larger than the dimension of
X\times_{K}Y
and let
r,r^{\prime}\geq 2d
. Let
S\in{\rm Perf}_{C}
.
The natural maps

\displaystyle\kappa:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(Y_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime})),

\displaystyle\kappa:\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})

are quasi-isomorphisms in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
.

Proof.

The pro-étale case follows from the syntomic one via the comparison quasi-isomorphism from Proposition
4.4
.

For the syntomic case, it is enough to show that on the level of
\varphi
-modules over
{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}
the corresponding map

\kappa:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{\bf B}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{S^{\flat}}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{\bf B}}(Y_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{\bf B}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))

is a quasi-isomorphism. Or that in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
, for
{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
, the induced map

\kappa^{[u,v]}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{[u,v]}}(Y_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{{[u,v]}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r+r^{\prime}))

is a quasi-isomorphism. But for that, as in the proof of Theorem
5.20
, it is enough to check that the base changes of
\kappa^{[u,v]}
to
{\bf B}^{\prime}[1/t]
and to
{\bf B}^{\prime}/t
are quasi-isomorphisms.

For the first base change, we use the quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))[1/t]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)[1/t]

to write

\kappa^{[u,v]}[1/t]:\quad({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(Y_{S},r^{\prime}))[1/t]\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},r+r^{\prime})[1/t].

This map is induced by the Hyodo-Kato pairing

\kappa^{[u,v]}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{S},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(Y_{S},r^{\prime})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{HK}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{S},r+r^{\prime}).

To check that this is a quasi-isomorphism we may pass to cohomology. Since
H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
is Fréchet (hence flat for the solid tensor product over
\breve{C}
), this reduces
to checking that the pairing

\bigoplus_{a=0}^{b}(H^{a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(H^{j-a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(Y_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}\to(H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}((X\times_{K}Y)_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\log})^{N=0}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}
.

Now,
using the exponential map as in the proof of Lemma
5.12
,
we can reduce to proving that the pairing

\bigoplus_{a=0}^{b}(H^{a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(H^{j-a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime})\to H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C}\times_{C}Y_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box}
. Or, that so is the pairing in
\breve{C}_{\Box}

\bigoplus_{a=0}^{b}H^{a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}H^{j-a}_{\operatorname{HK}}(Y_{C})\to H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C}\times_{C}Y_{C}).

But this follows from the following:

Lemma 5.35
.

(Hyodo-Kato Künneth formula)
Let
X,Y
be smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties over
C
. Then the canonical pairing

\kappa_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(Y)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X\times_{C}Y).

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}_{\varphi,N,{\mathcal{G}}_{K}}(\breve{C}_{\Box})
.

Proof.

This follows from the comparison (via the Hyodo-Kato morphism) with the Künneth formula for de Rham cohomology

\kappa_{\operatorname{dR}}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{C}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\times_{C}Y).

The latter clearly holds if both
X
and
Y
are Stein. For a general partially proper
X
and
Y
, we use coverings by a countable number (!) of Stein varieties, the fact that all the complexes in sight are bounded complexes of Fréchet spaces,
[
9
, Prop. 8.33]
, and the Stein case.
∎

For the base change to
R={\bf B}^{\prime}/t
, we get from the proof of Theorem
5.20
, compatible with products, quasi-isomorphisms in

{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime}_{\Box})
(
T=X,Y
,
s\geq 0
)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{{[u,v]}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(T_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq F^{s}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(T_{S}/{\bf B}^{\prime})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}R

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{T}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(T,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(s-i)[-i].

And the map
\kappa^{[u,v]}
can be identified with the map

\displaystyle\Big(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{X}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r-i)[-i]\Big)

\displaystyle\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{R}\Big(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{Y}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(Y,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r^{\prime}-i)[-i]\Big)

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d_{X}+d_{Y}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X\times_{K}Y,\Omega^{i})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r+r^{\prime}-i)[-i].

If
X,Y
are Stein, this map
in degree
i
is represented by the map

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d_{X}+d_{Y}}\Big(\Omega^{a}(X)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r-a)\Big)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{R}\Big(\Omega^{i-a}(Y)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r^{\prime}-i+a)\Big)\to\Omega^{i}(X\times_{K}Y)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}R(r+r^{\prime}-i).

And the latter map is a quasi-isomorphism in
R_{\Box}
by (
5.33
). If
X,Y
are general smooth partially proper rigid analytic varieties, we can reduce to the Stein case as in the proof of Lemma
5.35
.
∎

6.
Poincaré duality for
p
-adic geometric pro-étale cohomology

Finally, we are ready to state and prove pro-étale duality on the level of Topological Vector Spaces
(Theorem
6.8
). We descend it from the analogous Poincaré duality on the Fargues-Fontaine curve (Corollary
5.32
).

6.1.
Topological Vector Spaces

In this paper, the category of Topological Vector Spaces
(TVS’s for short) is the
\infty
-category of
\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}
-modules in the
\infty
-derived category

\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\rm Solid})
of topologically enriched presheaves
on
{\rm sPerf}_{C}
– the category of strictly totally disconnected affinoids over
C
– with values in solid abelian groups. We will denote it by
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
. This category was defined and studied in
[
19
]
. We will denote by

{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
the corresponding
\infty
-category where we forget the enrichment; the objects of this category will be called "topological presheaves".

We list the following properties:

Proposition 6.1
.

(
[
19
, Th. 1.1]
)

(1)

(Enriched fully-faithfulness)
The canonical functor from Vector Spaces
14
14

14

We call Vector Spaces (VS’s for short) the objects in the
\infty
-derived category

{\mathcal{D}}(\operatorname{Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})
of
\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}
-modules in the category of pro-étale sheaves on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
, the category of perfectoid affinoids over
C
.
to Topological Vector
Spaces

{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}:{\mathcal{D}}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})\to\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p})

tends to be fully faithful. More precisely, let
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})
be such that
{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{F}}\in\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}^{b}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p})
and
let
{\mathcal{G}}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{+}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})
. Then the canonical morphism in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p})

{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{C}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{C^{\rm top}}({\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{G}})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

(Fargues-Fontaine fully-faithfulness)
The
functor

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}:{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})\to\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p})

is fully faithful when restricted to perfect complexes. That is, for
{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}\in{\rm Perf}(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}})
, the natural map in
{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Mod}^{\rm cond}_{\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}(C)})

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},C^{\flat}})}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{C,{\bf Q}_{p}}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{G}})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

(3)

(Compatibility of the algebraic and topological projections)
The
functor

{\mathrm{R}}\tau^{\prime}_{*}:{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},C^{\flat}})\to{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})

is compatible with the functor
{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}
when restricted to nuclear sheaves. That is, the
following diagram commutes

\textstyle{{\rm Nuc}(X_{\operatorname{FF},C^{\flat}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\tau^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{{\bf Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{{\bf Q}}_{p}).}

6.2.
TVS-version of pro-étale cohomology presheaves

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
.
For
?=-,c
, we define the presheaves on
{\rm sPerf}_{C}
with values in
{\mathcal{D}}({\rm Solid})

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}):\quad S\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}).

The topology on pro-étale cohomology is induced via Čech procedure from
p
-adic topologies. Note that the values of these presheaves on
S
are actually in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})
.

Proposition 6.2
.

(1)

The presheaf
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
is naturally enriched:

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\in\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}).

(2)

There exists a natural quasi-isomorphism in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),

where the sheaf
{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
is the

algebraic version
15
15

15

Defined as
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
but with discrete topology objectwise.
of
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.

(3)

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety over
K
. There exists a natural quasi-isomorphism in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),\quad?=-,c.

(6.3)

Proof.

For claim (1), consider first the usual cohomology.
We want to define (a straighten version of) structure maps

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S\times T},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box}[T],{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})),

for
S\in{\rm sPerf}_{C}
and a profinite set
T
. By pro-étale descent, it suffices to construct, for a set
\{S_{i}\},i\in I
,
S_{i}\in{\rm sPerf}_{C}
, functorial structure maps

\prod_{I}\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}(S_{i}\times T)\to\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box}[T],\prod_{I}\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}(S_{i}))

or functorial maps

\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}(S_{i}\times T)\to\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box}[T],\underline{{\bf Q}}_{p}(S_{i}))

But these maps can be identified with the canonical isomorphisms (see
[
19
, Ex. 2.1]
)

\underline{{\mathcal{C}}(|S_{i}\times T|,{\bf Q}_{p})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}(T,\underline{{\mathcal{C}}(|S_{i}\times T|,{\bf Q}_{p})})}

To treat the compactly supported version
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
, recall that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}((\partial X)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})],

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}((\partial X)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{colim}_{Z\in\Phi_{X}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}((X\mathbin{\fgebackslash}Z)_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}).

This canonically induces the enrichment on the presheaf
{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.

Claim (2), from the above argument, is clear for the usual cohomology. Then it follows for compactly supported cohomology on the level of presheaves described by the algebraic version of (
6.4
) because the proof of the claim (3) below shows that these are actually sheaves.

For claim (3), by Proposition
6.1
and claim (2), it suffices to show that

{\mathrm{R}}\tau^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),\quad?=-,c.

Note that, by definition, we have as presheaves

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\tau^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{S\mapsto{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},\operatorname{L}\hskip-1.13809ptf^{*}_{S}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\{S\mapsto{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p})\}.

Hence it suffices to refer to Lemma
6.5
below.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

On
X_{\operatorname{FF},S^{\flat}}
we have a natural quasi-isomorphism of solid quasi-coherent sheaves

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}\hskip-1.13809ptf^{*}_{S}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}).

Proof.

It suffices to show that, for a compact rational interval
[u,v]\subset(0,\infty)
,
the canonical map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{C^{\flat}}}{\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}{\to}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{S},{\mathbb{B}}^{[u,v]})

is a quasi-isomorphism. Since tensor product commutes with colimits it suffices to do this for the usual cohomology.

By Theorem
4.7
, it suffices to show the same for the twisted Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies. Assume thus that
r\geq 2d

and let us start with the Hyodo-Kato cohomology. We want to show that the base change map
(for
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}:={\bf B}_{S^{\flat}}^{[u,v]}
)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{C},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)

is a quasi-isomorphism.
For that, we may pass to cohomology. Since
H^{j}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})
is Fréchet (hence flat for the solid tensor product over
\breve{C}
), this reduces
to checking that the base change, for
b\geq 0
,

(H^{b}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C,\log})^{N=0}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}\to(H^{b}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S,\log})^{N=0}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S,\Box}
.

Now,
using the exponential map as in the proof of Lemma
5.12
, we can reduce to proving that the base change

(H^{b}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}\to H^{b}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S,\Box}
. But this is clear.

We pass now to the de Rham cohomology. As above it suffices to show that the base change map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{C},r)\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{{[u,v]}}(X_{S},r)

is a quasi-isomorphism. But this reduces to showing that the base change maps

\displaystyle(\Omega^{i}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{s}))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}_{C}}{\bf B}^{\prime}_{S}\to\Omega^{i}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}(S)/t^{s})

are isomorphisms. And this is clear.
∎

6.3.
Topological Poincaré duality

Let
X
be a partially proper smooth variety over
K
of dimension
d
.
Let
i,j\geq 0
. We define a pairing in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(j))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(i+j-d)[-2d]

(6.6)

by inducing it from the compatible family of pairings in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p}(S)_{\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}(S)}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(j))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cup}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{S},{\bf Q}_{p}(i+j))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Tr}_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{{\bf Q}}_{p}(S)(i+j-d)[-2d],

where the trace map comes from the trace maps (
5.29
) via the fundamental exact sequence.
The fact that this pairing is compatible with the enrichments follows from the fact that it is induced by the algebraic pairing, we have Proposition
6.2
, and the projection functor
{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}
is lax monoidal.

The pairing in (
6.6
) induces a duality map in

\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\gamma_{X_{C}}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{{\bf Q}}_{p}).

(6.7)

Theorem 6.8
.

(Pro-étale duality)

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
of dimension
d
. The duality map
(
6.7
) is a quasi-isomorphism. In particular,
we have a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\gamma_{X_{C}}:\quad{{\mathrm{R}}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\bf Q}_{p}).

Proof.

(
\bullet
) Assume first that de Rham cohomology of
X
has finite rank. It suffices to show that, for
r,r^{\prime}\geq 2d,s=r+r^{\prime}-d
, the pairing in (
6.6
) induces a duality map in

\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\gamma_{X_{C}}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{{\bf Q}}_{p}(s)),

which is a quasi-isomorphism.
But we have the quasi-isomorphism

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},?}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

from Proposition
6.2
and the quasi-isomorphism
{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(i,i)\simeq{\bf Q}_{p}(i)
,
i\geq 0
.
Moreover, the functor
{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}
is lax monoidal and compatible with pro-étale traces, hence it suffices
to show that the duality map

\gamma_{X_{C}}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

is a quasi-isomorphism.

This map factorizes as

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}\gamma_{X_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))[2d],{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm can}

where we set

{\mathbb{D}}(-,-):={\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm QCoh(X_{\operatorname{FF},C^{\flat}})}(-,-)

The left slanted map is a quasi-isomorphism by the Poincaré duality on the Fargues -Fontaine curve from
Theorem
5.20
.
Hence it suffices to show that the canonical morphism

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

is a quasi-isomorphism. Or, by Proposition
4.4
, that so is the canonical morphism

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)).

Applying
{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(-,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))
and
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(-),{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))
to the distinguished triangle

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime}))\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime})

(6.9)

and identifying
{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)\simeq{\bf Q}_{p}(s)
,
we get compatible distinguished triangles

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\leftarrow{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\leftarrow{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r^{\prime})),{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\leftarrow{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\leftarrow{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\bf Q}_{p}(s)).

It suffices thus to show that the canonical morphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

\displaystyle\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\bf Q}_{p}(s)),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

\displaystyle\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime}),{\bf Q}_{p}(s))

are quasi-isomorphisms.

Since the solid quasi-coherent complexes
{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X,r^{\prime})
and
{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)
are perfect, the first quasi-isomorphism follows from Proposition
6.1
.
For the second quasi-isomorphism, since
{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X,r^{\prime})=i_{\infty,*}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C}/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+})/F^{r^{\prime}}
, it suffices to show that the natural morphism

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}(W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{i}),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(\iota_{\infty,*}(W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{i})),{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

(6.10)

is a quasi-isomorphism, for any space of compact type
W\in C_{K}
. By devissage we may assume that
i=1
.
Also, if we write
W\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{n}W_{n}
as a compact colimit of Smith spaces
W_{n}
over
K
we may assume that
W
is a Smith space over
K
. This is because

W^{*}
is then a compact limit of Banach spaces and such limits commute with solid tensors with Banach spaces yielding that both sides of (
6.10
) will change the colimit into a derived limit (see also the first claim in Lemma
6.12
).
Moreover, assuming that
W
is a Smith space over
K
, we can write
W=W_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}K
,
for a Smith space
W_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
over
{\bf Q}_{p}
.
Then we have
W^{*}=W^{*}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}K
.
Hence we may assume that
K={\bf Q}_{p}
in (
6.10
). To sum up, we need to show that the natural morphism

{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}C),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(\iota_{\infty,*}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}C)),{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

(6.11)

is a quasi-isomorphism, for any Smith space
W\in C_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
.

We will need the following computation:

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
s\in{\bf N}
. Let
W\in C_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
be a Smith space.

(1)

The canonical morphism in
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\operatorname{FF},C^{\flat}})

f_{1}:\quad W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}C),{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))

(6.13)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

The canonical morphisms in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}(C,{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\displaystyle f_{2}:\quad W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))),

\displaystyle f_{3}:\quad W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}\iota_{\infty,*}C\to{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}\iota_{\infty,*}C)

are quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

The case of the morphism
f_{3}
is clear. The morphism
f_{2}
can be written as the following composition of quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}(W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}\iota_{\infty,*}C(s-1)[-1])

\displaystyle\simeq W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(s-1)[-1]\simeq W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s)).

For the morphism
f_{1}
,
we can pass to the category
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\operatorname{FF}}_{C^{\flat},\Box})
. Set

{\bf B}^{\prime}:={\bf B}^{[u,v]}_{C^{\flat},{\rm an}}
. By Section
2.2.3
, it suffices to show that the canonical map

W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(C,{\bf B}^{\prime})\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}C,{\bf B}^{\prime})

is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime})
.
Or, since

{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(C,{\bf B}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}C(-1)[-1],

(6.14)

that the composition in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime})

W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}C(-1)[-1]\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}C,{\bf B}^{\prime})

(6.15)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

For that, we write the map (
6.15
) as a composition of quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf B}^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}C,{\bf B}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}{\bf B}^{\prime},{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(C,{\bf B}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}{\bf B}^{\prime},C)(-1)[-1]\simeq(W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}C)(-1)[-1].

Here, the first quasi-isomorphism is the internal tensor-hom adjunction; the second quasi-isomorphism follows from (
6.14
). The last quasi-isomorphism is induced by the following commutative diagram in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}{\bf B}^{\prime},C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf B}^{\prime}}(W{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}{\bf B}^{\prime},C)}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}(W,C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}(W,C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

(6.16)

where we have used that
W
is an internal projective object in solid
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules, and the fact that
{\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}(W,V)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}W^{*}{\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}}}V
, for a Fréchet space
V
over
{\bf Q}_{p}
(see
[
30
, Th. 3.40]
). We note here that the arrows in diagram (
6.16
) are
{\bf B}^{\prime}
-linear. This finishes the proof of the first claim of the lemma.
∎

By Lemma
6.12
, to show that the morphism (
6.11
) is a quasi-isomorphism it suffices to show that so is the natural morphism

W^{*}\otimes_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})

(6.17)

is a quasi-isomorphism. But this morphism factors as the composition

\textstyle{W^{*}\otimes_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\iota_{\infty,*}C,{\mathcal{O}}(s,s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))}
\textstyle{W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}

where the left quasi-isomorphism follows from Proposition
6.1
.
Hence it remains to show that the morphism
f_{0}
above is a quasi-isomorphism.

Since, by
[
19
, Lemma 3.27]
, we have

{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})),

we can rewrite the morphism
f_{0}
(untwisted by
s
) as the canonical morphism

W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})).

Or, since
{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{G}}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)[-1]
, as (the shift of) the canonical morphism

W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathbb{G}}_{a}).

It remains thus to show that this morphism is a quasi-isomorphism.

Let
S\in{\rm sPerf}_{C}
. We have quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle W^{*}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}^{\Box}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}({W},{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(S)),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathbb{G}}_{a})(S)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}_{\Box}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\bf Q}_{p}[h^{\rm top}_{S}]^{\Box},{\mathbb{G}}_{a})\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\bf Q}_{p}[h^{\rm top}_{S}]^{\Box},{\mathbb{G}}_{a})).

Here the first quasi-isomorphism follows
from the fact that
W
is a Smith space (hence an internal projective object in solid modules). This reduces us to showing that the canonical morphism

{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}^{\Box}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}({W},{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(S))\to{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}(\underline{W},{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\bf Q}_{p}[h^{\rm top}_{S}]^{\Box},{\mathbb{G}}_{a}))

is a quasi-isomorphism. But, since

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma({\rm Spa}(C)^{\rm top},{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\bf Q}_{p}[h^{\rm top}_{S}]^{\Box},{\mathbb{G}}_{a}))\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\bf Q}_{p}[h^{\rm top}_{S}]^{\Box},{\mathbb{G}}_{a})\simeq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(S),

this follows from

[
19
, Lemma 3.27]
.

(
\bullet
) For a general smooth partially proper variety
X
, we cover it with Stein varieties
\{X_{i}\},i\in I
, such that de Rham cohomology of each
X_{i}
is of finite rank. Then
we consider the associated Čech hypercovering
Y_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
X
and we compute in
\underline{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C),{\bf Q}_{p,\Box})

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Rlim}_{n}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\rm Rlim}_{n}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(Y_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(Y_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(Y_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\bf Q}_{p}),

as wanted. Here, the second quasi-isomorphism follows from the case of duality already proven.
∎

6.4.
Algebraic Poincaré duality

The topological Poincaré duality from Theorem
6.8
has an algebraic version, which we will now present.
Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
.
The algebraic analog of the pairing in (
6.6
) induces a duality map in

{{\mathcal{D}}}({\rm Spa}(C)_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}},{\bf Q}_{p})

\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm VS}({\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{{\bf Q}}_{p}).

(6.18)

Corollary 6.19
.

(Algebraic pro-étale duality)
Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid analytic variety over
K
of dimension
d
. The duality map
(
6.18
) is a quasi-isomorphism. In particular,
we have a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathcal{D}}({\bf Q}_{p})

\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}:\quad{{\mathrm{R}}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{\rm VS}({\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\bf Q}_{p}).

(6.20)

Proof.

Apply the projection functor
{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}
to the map (
6.18
). We obtain the horizontal map in the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm VS}({\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{{\bf Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{X_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{C^{\rm top}}({\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm alg}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}{{\bf Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{C^{\rm top}}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))[2d],{{\bf Q}}_{p}).}

The top right vertical arrow is a quasi-isomorphism by Proposition
6.1
; the map
\gamma_{X_{C}}
is a quasi-isomorphism by Theorem
6.8
. It follows that so is the map
{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}
.

Now, we have a functor
{\mathrm{R}}\eta_{*}:{\mathcal{D}}^{\rm psh}({\rm sPerf}_{C},{\rm Mod}^{\rm cond}_{{\bf Q}_{p}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\rm psh}({\rm sPerf}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
from topological presheaves to algebraic presheaves ("evaluation at
*
") such that
{\mathrm{R}}\eta_{*}{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\iota_{*}
, the canonical forgetful functor from sheaves to presheaves (see
[
19
, Sec. 2.1.2]
for details).
Applying
{\mathrm{R}}\eta_{*}
to
{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{*}\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}
we get that the map
{\mathrm{R}}\iota_{*}\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}
is a quasi-isomorphism and hence so is the map
\gamma^{\rm alg}_{X_{C}}
(after applying the sheafification functor), as wanted.
∎

6.5.
Verdier exact sequence

In the Stein case, the duality (
6.20
) takes a simple form.

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
X
be a smooth Stein variety over
K
. Let
i\geq 0
.
There exists a short exact sequence in
TVS
’s

0\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i+1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

In particular, there exists a short exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

0\to\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i+1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

Proof.

The second claim follows easily from the first claim. For the first claim,
having Theorem
6.8
and the spectral sequence

E^{a,b}_{2}={\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{-b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\Rightarrow H^{a+b}({\mathrm{R}}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p}))

it suffices to show that

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})=0,\quad a\geq 2.

(6.22)

Let
r\geq 2d
. By Proposition
4.4
, we have the quasi-isomorphism

{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{syn},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

which, by (
3.11
) and (
3.3
), yields an exact sequence

\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{b-1}}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{D}}{\mathbb{R}}^{b-1}_{c}(X_{C},r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{H}}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{K}}^{b}_{c}(X_{C},r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{b}}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{D}}{\mathbb{R}}^{b}_{c}(X_{C},r)\to\cdots

(6.23)

where we set

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{K}}^{b}_{c}(X_{C},r)

\displaystyle:=(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{D}}{\mathbb{R}}^{b}_{c}(X_{C},r)

\displaystyle:=H^{b-d}(H^{d}_{c}(X,{\mathcal{O}})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{r})\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{1})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{r-1})\to\cdots\to H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{d})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/t^{r-d}))

We used here the natural isomorphisms (
?=-,c
)

H^{b}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{HK},?}(X_{C},r)\simeq\mathbb{HK}^{b}_{?}(X_{C},r),\quad H^{b}{\mathrm{R}}\tau_{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{dR},?}(X_{C},r)\simeq\mathbb{DR}^{b}_{?}(X_{C},r),

that are compatible with the Hyodo-Kato map and which
follow immediately from the computations in the proof of Proposition
6.2
.

Let
i\geq 0
. We will need to understand the maps
\iota_{i}
from (
6.23
) better. We have a commutative diagram in
{\rm TVS}
, where we set
s=r-i+d-1
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}t^{s}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{HK}^{i}_{c}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{i}}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{DR}^{i}_{c}(X_{C},r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{i}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{i}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

(6.24)

Here, The middle row comes from (
3.4
); the top row from
[
1
, Lemma 8.1]
.
We have defined
{\mathbb{E}}^{i}:=\operatorname{coker}(\iota_{i})
;

\mathbb{E}^{i}_{1}
is the image of
(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}
under the map
f_{1}
,
and
\mathbb{E}^{i}_{2}
is the cokernel of the map
f_{2}
. The rows are exact; so are the first and the second columns. Moreover, the map
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}t^{s}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\to(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}
factors as

(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{{\Box}}_{\breve{C}}t^{s}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{can}}}(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Im}\,d)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a},

(6.25)

where the second map is an injection with quotient
(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Ker}\,d)\otimes^{{\Box}}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}
. It follows that we have an exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{V}\to\mathbb{E}^{i}_{1}\to(H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{i-d})/{\rm Ker}\,d)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}t^{s}\mathbb{G}_{a}\to 0,

(6.26)

where
\mathbb{V}
is a (topological) BC.

(
\bullet
)
Finite rank case.
Assume first that the de Rham cohomology has finite rank.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}^{i}:=\ker(\iota_{i}).

It is a BC: this is because the map
\iota_{i}
factors through de Rham cohomology, which is a BC (see (
6.25
)).
We have the exact sequence

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{A}}^{b},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{E}}^{b-1},{\bf Q}_{p}).

(6.27)

Hence it suffices to show that, for
a\geq 2
, we have

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{A}}^{b},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,\quad{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{E}}^{b-1},{\bf Q}_{p})=0.

(6.28)

This is clear for
{\mathbb{A}}^{b}
because it is a BC and we have
[
19
, Ex. 4.29]
. For
{\mathbb{E}}^{b-1}
, we use the exact sequence (
6.26
). It follows that
it suffices to show that, for
a\geq 2
, we have

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{V},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,\quad{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,

where we set
W=H^{d}_{c}(X,\Omega^{b-1-d})/{\rm Ker}\,d
.
Since
\mathbb{V}
is a BC, the first equality is clear.
For the second one, if
K={\bf Q}_{p}
, we have in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
(see
[
19
, Lemma 3.27]
)

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq W^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,

as wanted. We used here that
W
is of compact type.

For a general
K
, assume first that
W
is a Smith space. Then we can write
W=W_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\otimes^{\operatorname{L}}_{{\bf Q}_{p},\Box}K
, for a Smith space
W_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
over
{\bf Q}_{p}
.
It follows that the canonical morphism

W^{*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})

is an isomorphism.
For a general
W
, write
W\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{n}W_{n}
as a compact colimit of Smith spaces
W_{n}
over
K
and note that

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(W\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(W_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}).

This is because
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a-1}_{\rm TVS}(W_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
: this follows from the Smith case for
a\geq 3
; for
a=2
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}(W^{*}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}(W^{*}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a})\simeq({\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}W^{*}_{n})\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a}=0.

The last isomorphism follows from the fact that
\{W_{n}^{*}\}
is a pro-system of Banach spaces with dense transition maps. The penultimate isomorphism follows from the following fact
16
16

16

Probably well-known but we did not find a reference.
:

Lemma 6.29
.

Let
\{V_{n}\}
be a set of Banach spaces over
K
and let
V
be a Banach space over
K
. Then the canonical map

V\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\prod_{n}V_{n}\to\prod_{n}(V\otimes^{\Box}_{K}V_{n})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Write
V=\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}({\bf Z}[T],K)
, for a profinite set
T
. Then

\displaystyle V\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\prod_{n}V_{n}

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}({\bf Z}[T],K)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\prod_{n}V_{n}\simeq\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}({\bf Z}[T],\prod_{n}V_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{n}\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}({\bf Z}[T],V_{n})\simeq\prod_{n}(\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}({\bf Z}[T],K)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}V_{n})\simeq\prod_{n}(V\otimes^{\Box}_{K}V_{n}).

The second isomorphism follows from the fact that the product
\prod_{n}V_{n}
is a nuclear
K
-vector space and the penultimate one from the fact that so is every
V_{n}
.
∎

(
\bullet
)
General case.
For a general smooth Stein variety
X
over
K
, we cover
X
with an exhaustive sequence
\{X_{n}\}
of Stein varieties with finite dimensional de Rham cohomologies. We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p}).

This yields the exact sequence

0\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a-1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

Hence, by the above,
{\mathcal{E}xt}^{a}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})=0
, for
a\geq 3
. For
a=2
, we have

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{2}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p}).

It suffices thus to show that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})=0.

From the exact sequence (
6.27
) and isomorphisms (
6.28
), we get
the exact sequence (for
X_{n}
)

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{A}^{b}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

This yields the exact sequence

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{A}}^{b}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{H}^{b}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{n,C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0.

Since
{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{A}}^{b}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})
is a BC we have
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{A}}^{b}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
as we have Mittag-Leffler in this setting. It suffices thus to show that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})=0
.

But from diagram (
6.24
), we see that it suffices to show that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{i,n},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,\quad i=1,2.

For
i=2
this is clear because
{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{2,n},{\bf Q}_{p})
is a BC (because
\mathbb{E}^{b-1}_{2,n}
is the cokernel of the map
\mathbb{HK}^{b-1}_{c}(X_{C},r)\to H^{b-1}_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X)\otimes^{\Box}_{K}({\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{s})
, hence a BC). It remains to treat the case
i=1
. For that we use the exact sequence (
6.26
). Since
\mathbb{V}_{n}
is a BC, it suffices to show that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,

where
W_{n}=H^{d}_{c}(X_{n},\Omega^{b-1-d})/{\rm Ker}\,d
.
Note that
W_{n}\hookrightarrow H^{d}_{c}(X_{n},\Omega^{b-d})=:W^{\prime}_{n}
. Hence we have the surjection

{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W^{\prime}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}).

It suffices thus to show that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W^{\prime}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0.

(6.30)

We claim that the canonical morphism

W^{\prime,*}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W^{\prime}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})

is an isomorphism. Indeed, we can write
W^{\prime}_{n}=\operatorname{colim}_{m}W^{\prime}_{n,m}
as a compact colimit of Smith spaces and argue as above using the fact that

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(W^{\prime}_{n,m}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq W^{\prime}_{n,m,*}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\rm TVS}(\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p})=0.

Hence we have a functorial in
n
isomorphism

W^{\prime,*}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\simeq{\mathcal{E}xt}^{1}_{\rm TVS}(W^{\prime}_{n}\otimes^{\Box}_{K}\mathbb{G}_{a},{\bf Q}_{p}).

Since we have
W^{\prime,*}_{n}\simeq\Omega^{2d-b}(X_{n})
,
(
6.30
) holds because the pro-system
\{\Omega^{2d-b}(X_{n})\}
,
n\in{\bf N}
, is equivalent to a pro-system of Banach spaces
\{V_{n}\}
with dense transition maps.
∎

6.6.
Examples

We will discuss in some detail here dualities for Stein curves and Drinfeld spaces.

6.6.1.
Proper varieties

Let
X
be a smooth proper rigid analytic variety over
K
. Then, since pro-étale cohomology of
X_{C}
is finite dimensional over
{\bf Q}_{p}
, Corollary
6.21
yields a duality isomorphism

{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{H}^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d))^{*}.

A result known by the work of Zavyalov
[
34
]
and Mann
[
29
]
.

6.6.2.
Stein curves

Let
X
be a geometrically connected smooth Stein curve over
K
.
From comparison theorems (see
[
22
, Th. 6.14]
) we get the following isomorphism and a short exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\bf Q}_{p},

(6.31)

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathcal{O}}(X_{C})/C\to

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\to(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C})\otimes^{\Box}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p}\to 0

They lift to TVS’s. Similarly, for compactly supported cohomology, by
[
1
, Sec. 7.2]
, we get the following isomorphism and a short exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\simeq(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\Box}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=1},

\displaystyle\to(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\Box}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{2}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}}H^{1}{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2)\to H^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(2))\to{\bf Q}_{p}(1)\to 0,

where we set
{\rm DR}_{c}(X_{C},2):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR},c}(X_{C},{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{2}
. We note that, if the de Rham cohomology of
X
is of finite rank over
K
, then
(H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})\otimes^{\Box}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=1}
is a finite rank
{\bf Q}_{p}
-vector space because the slopes of Frobenius
on
H^{1}_{\operatorname{HK},c}(X_{C})
are
\geq 0
(see
[
1
, Remark 7.10]
).
Again, everything lifts to TVS’s.

By Corollary
6.21
we get
a short exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

0\to\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i+1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

Hence, using the above computations and
d=1
since
X
is a curve,
we get the following isomorphism and a short exact sequence in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1)),{\bf Q}_{p})

(6.32)

\displaystyle 0\to\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{p}(1)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to{H}^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})\to\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(1)),{\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

It is tempting to think that the exact sequence in (
6.32
) recovers the exact sequence in (
6.31
) but this is not the case: if the de Rham cohomology of
X
is of finite rank over
K
, the term on the right in (
6.32
) is a finite rank
{\bf Q}_{p}
-vector space while the term on the right in (
6.31
) will, in general, have a nontrivial
C
-part.

6.6.3.
Drinfeld space

Let
K
be a finite extension of
{\bf Q}_{p}
and let
d\geq 1
. Let
{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{K}
be the Drinfeld space of dimension
d
over
K
.
By
[
14
, Th. 1.3]
,
[
1
, Lemma 8.13]
, we have exact sequences in
{\bf Q}_{p,\Box}
(
i\geq 0
)

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{i-1}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C})/\ker d\to

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\to{\rm Sp}_{i}({\bf Q}_{p})^{*}\to 0

(6.33)

\displaystyle 0\to H^{d}_{c}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C},\Omega^{i-d-1})/\ker d\to

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i-d))\to{\rm Sp}_{2d-i}({\bf Q}_{p})\to 0

Here
{\rm Sp}_{i}({\bf Q}_{p})
denotes the generalized locally constant Steinberg
{\bf Q}_{p}
-representation of
{\rm GL}_{d+1}(K)
(see
[
14
, Sec. 5.2.1]
for a definition). Hence the terms on the right in (
6.33
) are nuclear Fréchet and of compact type over
{\bf Q}_{p}
, respectively. It follows that

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}({\mathcal{H}}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{{\rm{Hom}}}_{\rm TVS}({\rm Sp}_{i}({\bf Q}_{p})(i),{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq{\rm Sp}_{i}({\bf Q}_{p})^{*}(-i),

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{2d-i+1}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t},c}({\mathcal{H}}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(d)),{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\rm TVS}((H^{d}_{c}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C},\Omega^{d-i})/\ker d)(i-1),{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq(H^{d}_{c}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C},\Omega^{d-i})/\ker d)^{*}(-i)\simeq(\Omega^{i-1}({\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{C})/\ker d)(-i).

The last quasi-isomorphism uses Serre’s duality (see
[
1
, Remark 8.11]
for details).

Hence, in this example, the duality sequence from Corollary
6.21
does transfer the compact support fundamental exact sequence into the usual fundamental exact sequence.

Remark 6.34
.

The case of affine spaces and tori is similar to the case of Drinfeld space
but simpler since the Hyodo-Kato terms on the right in the fundamental exact sequences are actually finite dimensional over
{\bf Q}_{p}
.
See
[
14
, Prop. 4.17]
,
[
1
, Sec. 7.1]
for the shape of these fundamental exact sequences.
